### 163
### APPENDIX II

An <term>exorcist</term> <note>original: "exorciser"; a ritual specialist trained to drive away or control spirits.</note> is sent for. He arrives and studies the layout of the house, the positioning of the doors and windows, and other features. Having gained a thorough knowledge of the premises, he is able to determine the path by which the devil enters and exits. A suitable corner is selected according to the rules of <term>sorcery</term>, and an iron nail is driven into the earth at that location. The devil is believed to be bound down by this act, and the master of the house feels he no longer has anything to fear from demonic influences.

To cast out a devil that has possessed a man or a woman, the following method is very commonly used. A wooden image of the person under the power of the evil spirit is crafted, and a square hole is cut into it just above the navel. The wood selected for this purpose is usually that of the <term>palamaram</term> <note>original: "palamaram"; the *Alstonia scholaris*, often called the "Devil Tree" or "Milkwood pine" due to its milky sap and traditional associations with spirits.</note>. This is done according to the rules of magic. Then, through the recitation of certain mysterious spells, the "essence" or life-force of the person suffering from the illness is transferred into the wooden image. The purpose of cutting a hole in the image seems to be to create an opening or entrance through which the person’s essence can pass. There may also be another belief at play: that such a ritual image should not be physically perfect in every part. The image is then taken to a tree that produces plenty of milky sap and is nailed to it. The trees chosen for such purposes are the <term>palamaram</term>, the <term>arayalmaram</term> <note>original: "arayalmaram"; the *Ficus religiosa* or Sacred Fig (Peepal) tree.</note>, or the <term>pezhumaram</term> <note>original: "pezhumaram"; the *Careya arborea*, known as the Slow Match Tree.</note>. The spirit is then thought to leave the person and inhabit the tree instead.

In the <term>Madras Museum</term> <note>Now known as the Government Museum, Chennai.</note>, there are two large wooden images, over five feet high, which are studded all over with wooden nails. The first is a life-sized, roughly carved female figure with its feet turned backwards <note>A common feature of spirits and demons in Indian folklore, indicating their supernatural or inverted nature.</note>. It was carved from the wood of the <term>Devil Tree</term> <note>original: "alstoria scholaris"; a botanical misspelling of *Alstonia scholaris*.</note> and was washed ashore at <term>Calicut</term> <note>original: "Calicut"; now Kozhikode, a major city in Kerala.</note> in 1903. It likely originated from the <term>Laccadive Islands</term> <note>original: "Laccadive islands"; now the Union Territory of Lakshadweep.</note>, where some residents are renowned as <term>necromancers</term> <note>Practitioners who communicate with or control the spirits of the dead.</note>. The figure probably represented a woman possessed by an evil spirit. Through magic rites and the driving in of nails, the people believed they had imprisoned the spirit within the image, and they then threw it...

# GLOSSARY OF INDIAN TERMS

*Mādigā community* <note>original: "Mādigās"</note>—the lowest section of the <term>Outcastes</term> <note>historically, social groups excluded from the four-tier Hindu caste system</note> in the <term>Telugu country</term> <note>the region of modern Andhra Pradesh and Telangana</note>, 28.
*Mālā community* <note>original: "Mālās"</note>—a large group of Outcastes in the Telugu country, 41.
*sacred text* <note>original: "mantram"</note>—a sacred text or prayer, 92.
*death-atonement* <note>original: "maranada bali"</note>—a sacrifice or offering related to death, 88.
*neem tree* <note>original: "margosa"</note>—the neem tree, often considered sacred in village rituals, 37.
*foreigner* <note>original: "mleccha"</note>—a term for a foreigner or non-Hindu, 19.
*auspicious moment* <note>original: "muhūrtam"</note>—a specifically timed moment, 132.
*hut cluster* <note>original: "mund"</note>—a group of traditional huts, 61.
*village magistrate* <note>original: "munsiff"</note>—a local village official or judge, 57.
*respectful greeting* <note>original: "namaskāram"</note>—a gesture of obeisance or respectful salutation, 66.
*temple dancers* <note>original: "nautch-girls"</note>—dancing girls attached to Hindu temples for ritual performances, 21.
*Mālā priest* <note>original: "Pambala"</note>—a hereditary priest belonging to the Mālā community, 58.
*fifth-class Outcaste* <note>original: "Pānchama"</note>—a person belonging to the Outcaste groups, 19.
*ceremonial booth* <note>original: "pandāl"</note>—a temporary shelter or decorated booth, 37.
*religious beggars* <note>original: "Panikas"</note>—mendicants who live on charity for religious reasons, 86, 87.
*Tamil Outcastes* <note>original: "Pariahs"</note>—the principal group of Outcastes in the Tamil-speaking regions, 14.
*great* <note>original: "pedda"</note>—meaning large or great, 70.
*raised platform* <note>original: "pial"</note>—a platform or porch at the front of a house, 81.
*blessed offering* <note>original: "prasādam"</note>—food or gifts given as a sign of divine grace, 64.
*act of worship* <note>original: "pūjā"</note>—ritual worship, 18.
*worship leader* <note>original: "pūjāri"</note>—one who conducts the rituals of worship, a ministrant, 18.

<column-break/>

*progeny sacrifice* <note>original: "puthrayāgam"</note>—a sacrificial ritual performed to obtain a child, 31.
*demon* <note>original: "rakshatha"</note>—a demonic or malevolent spirit, 122.
*village headman* <note>original: "reddy"</note>—a local village magistrate or leader, 71.
*sacred seed* <note>original: "rudrāksha"</note>—a kind of berry or seed used for prayer beads, 129.
*peasant farmer* <note>original: "ryot"</note>—a small-scale farmer, 52.
*divine power* <note>original: "śakti"</note>—energy or power, usually associated with female deities, 29.
*sacred scriptures* <note>original: "śāstras"</note>—the traditional Hindu religious and legal books, 84.
*full prostration* <note>original: "shashthāngam"</note>—the act of bowing with eight parts of the body touching the ground, 74.
*the innocent ones* <note>original: "siddhalu"</note>—referring to those who are pure or innocent, 52.
*laboring caste* <note>original: "Śūdra"</note>—the fourth and final level of the traditional Hindu caste hierarchy, 19.
*spear* <note>original: "śūlam"</note>—a sacrificial spear or spike, 40.
*regional magistrate* <note>original: "tahsildar"</note>—the chief magistrate of a specific sub-division within a district, 57.
*marriage token* <note>original: "tāli"</note>—a gold disk or ornament worn as a symbol of marriage, 27, 132.
*village guard* <note>original: "taliāri"</note>—a servant of the village, 72.
*spiritual discipline* <note>original: "tapas"</note>—acts of austerity or meditation, 84.
*native drum* <note>original: "tom-tom"</note>—a traditional drum used in ceremonies, 38.
*village watchman* <note>original: "toti"</note>—a person tasked with watching over the village, 78.
*divine triad* <note>original: "Trimūrti"</note>—the trinity of main Hindu gods (Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva), 24.
*trident* <note>original: "triśūla"</note>—a three-pronged spear or trident, 40 n. 2.
*yellow dye* <note>original: "turmeric"</note>—a yellow spice and dye used in rituals, 48.
*divine mount* <note>original: "vāhana"</note>—the animal or vehicle of a deity, 90.
*merchant caste* <note>original: "Vaiśya"</note>—the third level of the traditional Hindu caste hierarchy, 19.
*animal sacrifice* <note>original: "vēta"</note>—the act of making a sacrifice, 70.
*village scavenger* <note>original: "vetty"</note>—a person responsible for cleaning or scavenging in a village, 56.
*hero spirit* <note>original: "vīran"</note>—a hero or warrior spirit, 33.
*cosmic age* <note>original: "yuga"</note>—a vast era or age of time, 132.
*landowner* <note>original: "zamindar"</note>—a prominent landowner or tax collector, 57.

<summary>This page contains a glossary of South Indian terms, ranging from social groups and administrative roles to religious rituals and artifacts.</summary>
